          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. In continuation of previous orders, the High-Powered Enquiry  
Committee (HPEC) submitted its status report dated 06.10.2026. It  
is informed that the first meeting of the Committee was convened on  
01.09.2026 and thereafter a space to operate as the Secretariat has  
been identified, where necessary repair work is ongoing. Mr.  
Srinivasa Kammath R, a retired Joint Registrar (Law) from the  

--- Page 18 ---
11
National Human Rights Commission (NHRC), has been appointed as the  
Member Secretary of the Committee.
2. It is pointed out that the HPEC has vide its letter dated  
13.09.2026 made the following requests to the Ministry of Home  
Affairs (MHA): (i) Server of adequate capacity to be installed in  
the server room being made ready in the proposed office; (ii) a  
suitable portal developed through NIC to facilitate the aggrieved  
persons to upload their complaints; (iii) a new generator of  
adequate load bearing capacity installed at premises allotted; and  
(iv) minimum Secretariat staff comprising of 01 IT professional, 01  
Section Officer, 02 Assistant Section Officers, 02 Data Entry  
Operators, 02 Stenographers, 01 Hindi Translator and 03 Multi-
Tasking Staff (MTS).
3. With respect to the above urgent requirements of the HPEC, the  
learned Solicitor General of India assured that if any facility is  
not available so far, the needful shall be done without any delay. 
4. The  status  report  further  reveals  that  the  National  
Informatics Centre (NIC) has been engaged for the design and  
development of the portal and the two standard templates, one each  
for the aggrieved protesters and another for aggrieved police  
personnel, have been provided to the NIC along with relevant  
materials. The NIC has been instructed to ensure strict provisions  
for anonymity and confidentiality of complaints, evidence, and  
statements so as to protect vulnerable witnesses and keep their  
identities undisclosed.
5. We direct the NIC, through the MHA, to design and develop the  
portal according to the HPEC's requirements, ensuring all specified  

--- Page 19 ---
12
witness protection measures are integrated. The Committee informed  
the Court that the portal, fully equipped with all necessary  
features, is complete and scheduled for launch next week. Public  
notices will subsequently be published in major national newspapers  
to widely announce its launch.
6. As  highlighted  in  the  status  report,  certain  essential  
facilities  are  still  required  for  the  smooth  and  seamless  
functioning of the HPEC. The MHA is directed to take necessary  
steps to provide these facilities.
7. Dr. Monika Gusain, learned Senior Counsel and Amicus Curiae 
representing the HPEC, stated that she will inform the committee  
that  first  Action  Taken  Report  in  terms  of  the  priorities  
highlighted in our order dated 10.09.2026 be submitted for further  
consideration.  
8. Counter affidavit, if any, be filed within three weeks.
9. List on 02.11.2026.
T.P.(Crl.) No. 836/2026
10. Since the matter pending before the Delhi High Court already  
stands transferred to this Court, we request the Hon'ble Chief  
Justice of the Delhi High Court to ensure the immediate transfer of  
records to this Court's registry, if already not transferred. The  
Secretary General of this Court will coordinate with the Registrar  
General of the Delhi High Court to facilitate a prompt transfer  
without delay.
(NITIN TALREJA)                            (PREETHI DILEEP KUMAR)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        DY. REGISTRAR

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. In continuation of previous orders, the High-Powered Enquiry  
Committee (HPEC) submitted its status report dated 06.10.2026. It  
is informed that the first meeting of the Committee was convened on  
01.09.2026 and thereafter a space to operate as the Secretariat has  
been identified, where necessary repair work is ongoing. Mr.  
Srinivasa Kammath R, a retired Joint Registrar (Law) from the  
National Human Rights Commission (NHRC), has been appointed as the  
Member Secretary of the Committee.
2. It is pointed out that the HPEC has vide its letter dated  
13.09.2026 made the following requests to the Ministry of Home  
Affairs (MHA): (i) Server of adequate capacity to be installed in  

--- Page 30 ---
23
the server room being made ready in the proposed office; (ii) a  
suitable portal developed through NIC to facilitate the aggrieved  
persons to upload their complaints; (iii) a new generator of  
adequate load bearing capacity installed at premises allotted; and  
(iv) minimum Secretariat staff comprising of 01 IT professional, 01  
Section Officer, 02 Assistant Section Officers, 02 Data Entry  
Operators, 02 Stenographers, 01 Hindi Translator and 03 Multi-
Tasking Staff (MTS).
3. With respect to the above urgent requirements of the HPEC, the  
learned Solicitor General of India assured that if any facility is  
not available so far, the needful shall be done without any delay. 
4. The  status  report  further  reveals  that  the  National  
Informatics Centre (NIC) has been engaged for the design and  
development of the portal and the two standard templates, one each  
for the aggrieved protesters and another for aggrieved police  
personnel, have been provided to the NIC along with relevant  
materials. The NIC has been instructed to ensure strict provisions  
for anonymity and confidentiality of complaints, evidence, and  
statements so as to protect vulnerable witnesses and keep their  
identities undisclosed.
5. We direct the NIC, through the MHA, to design and develop the  
portal according to the HPEC's requirements, ensuring all specified  
witness protection measures are integrated. The Committee informed  
the Court that the portal, fully equipped with all necessary  
features, is complete and scheduled for launch next week. Public  
notices will subsequently be published in major national newspapers  
to widely announce its launch.

--- Page 31 ---
24
6. As  highlighted  in  the  status  report,  certain  essential  
facilities  are  still  required  for  the  smooth  and  seamless  
functioning of the HPEC. The MHA is directed to take necessary  
steps to provide these facilities.
7. Dr. Menaka Guruswamy, learned Senior Counsel representing the  
HPEC, stated that she will inform the committee that first Action  
Taken Report in terms of the priorities highlighted in our order  
dated 10.09.2026 be submitted for further consideration.  
8. Counter affidavit, if any, be filed within three weeks.
9. List on 02.11.2026.
T.P.(Crl.) No. 836/2026
10. Since the matter pending before the Delhi High Court already  
stands transferred to this Court, we request the Hon'ble Chief  
Justice of the Delhi High Court to ensure the immediate transfer of  
records to this Court's registry, if already not transferred. The  
Secretary General of this Court will coordinate with the Registrar  
General of the Delhi High Court to facilitate a prompt transfer  
without delay.
(NITIN TALREJA)                            (PREETHI DILEEP KUMAR)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        DY. REGISTRAR

ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                       ASSISTANT REGISTRAR

--- Page 50 ---
IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL ORIGINAL JURISDICTION
WRIT PETITION(C) NO.1224/2026
TAPASMITA SUTAR PETITIONER(S)
                              VERSUS
UNION OF INDIA & ORS. RESPONDENT(S)
O R D E R
1. The Petitioner, who is presently serving as a Bench
Clerk in the District Judiciary, Cuttack, has filed the
instant Writ Petition seeking a direction to the Respondent-
authorities  to  recognize  her  service  in  the  District
Judiciary towards the minimum period of practice prescribed
for  eligibility  to  appear  in  the  Civil  Judge  (Junior
Division) Examination.
2. The Petitioner was initially appointed as a Junior
Clerk-cum-Copyist  on  30.01.2012  and  was  subsequently
promoted to the post of Bench Clerk Grade-III on 14.08.2019.
While serving in this post, she completed her LL.B. degree
in 2021. By the Advertisement dated 02.03.2024, the Odisha
Public Service Commission notified 34 vacancies to the post
of Civil Judge (Junior Division) and the Petitioner applied
for the same. She qualified both the preliminary and the
main examinations. However, after the viva voce, her name
did not appear in the final list of selected candidates.
3. Shortly thereafter, this Court passed the Judgment dated
1

--- Page 51 ---
20.05.2025 in  All India Judges’ Association and Ors. v.
Union  of  India  and  Ors. ,  Writ  Petition  (Civil)  No.
1022/1989, whereby a minimum of three years’ practice at the
Bar was restored as an eligibility criterion for appointment
to the post of Civil Judge (Junior Division). While doing
so, it was further directed in paragraph 89 as follows: 
“(vii)…We further direct that the experience of
the candidates which they have gained while
working as Law Clerks with any of the Judges or
Judicial Officers in the country should also be
considered while calculating their total number
of years of practice…”
4. Pursuant  to  the  Judgment  dated  20.05.2025,  the
Government of Odisha notified the Odisha Superior Judicial
Service and Odisha Judicial Service (Second Amendment) Rules,
2025, whereby Rule 18(1)(a), as amended, prescribed the then-
applicable practice requirement of three years and included
within it the period spent as a Law Clerk. Thereafter, the
Odisha Public Service Commission issued an Advertisement
dated 30.04.2026 inviting applications for 78 vacancies in
the post of Civil Judge (Junior Division).
5. Aggrieved by the Judgment dated 20.05.2025, several
parties sought its clarification or modification through Writ
Petition (Civil) No. 1110/2025, Review Petition (Civil) Diary
No. 37749/2025, Review Petition (Civil) No. 421/2026, and
Review Petition (Civil) No. 420/2026. While hearing these
matters, this Court considered it appropriate to obtain the
views and suggestions of all the High Courts, as well as of
Law Universities and National Law Schools.
2

--- Page 52 ---
6. In view of the continuing uncertainty about how the
experience  requirement  would  operate,  and  how  relevant
experience acquired within the judicial system would be
treated, the Petitioner filed I.A. No. 212984/2026 in Writ
Petition (Civil) No. 1110/2025, seeking consideration of her
individual claim for recognition and computation of her
service in the District Judiciary. During the hearing, this
Court orally advised her to pursue her claim by way of a
separate Writ Petition.
7. Meanwhile, the proceedings seeking modification of the
Judgment dated 20.05.2025 continued and culminated in the
Judgment dated 21.08.2026 passed in Bhumika Trust v. Union of
India and Ors. , Writ Petition (Civil) No. 1110/2025 and
connected matters. Having considered the submissions advanced
and the material placed on record, this Court observed that
the Judgment dated 20.05.2025 did not require reconsideration
on the fundamental question of whether some prior exposure to
the working of courts is desirable for a person seeking entry
into  the  judicial  services,  and  held  that  the  reasons
assigned for restoring the practice requirement retained
considerable force. The issue under consideration was thus
narrowed to whether such exposure necessarily requires three
years of conventional practice at the Bar, and how the
requirement is to be implemented as a condition precedent to
appearing in the examination.
8. On these questions, this Court held that the requirement
of experience must bear a reasonable relationship to the
3

--- Page 53 ---
object it seeks to achieve, and that the passage of time at
the Bar, while a significant component, may not be the sole
determinant of meaningful courtroom experience. Accordingly,
the Judgment dated 20.05.2025 was modified to the following
extent: 
“73. In light of the above discussion, we
consider it appropriate to issue the following
directions,  which  would  apply  to  all
recruitment  notification/advertisement(s)
issued to the post of Civil Judge (Junior
Division) in the transitional period, i.e.,
notification/advertisement(s) issued after the
Judgement under Review up to 31.03.2027:
(i) All law graduates shall be eligible to
apply, notwithstanding the three-year
practice requirement. Having regard to
the fact that more than one year has
elapsed since the Judgment under Review
was pronounced, such candidates shall,
for the purposes of their applications,
be deemed to have completed one year of
active  practice.  They  shall  not  be
required  to  furnish  a  separate
Certificate of Practice in support of
the said deemed period; 
(ii) Candidates  selected  pursuant  to  the
aforesaid  recruitment  shall,  upon
appointment, be designated as “Trainee
Judicial Officers” and shall undergo a
compulsory  period  of  one  year  of
intensive  training  at  the  concerned
State  Judicial  Academy.  This  period
shall be treated as equivalent to one
year of practice at the Bar for the
purposes of the three-year requirement;
(iii) During the period of such training, the
Trainee Judicial Officers shall be paid
a fixed emolument equivalent to one
half of the remuneration payable to a
Judicial Magistrate First Class in the
concerned  State.  They  shall,  in
addition, be entitled to the facilities
and  other  benefits  ordinarily  made
available to trainees at the concerned
State Judicial Academy;
(iv) Upon  successful  completion  of  the
aforesaid  training,  the  Trainee
4

--- Page 54 ---
Judicial  Officers  shall  undergo  a
further  period  of  one  year  of
structured Law Clerkship. The first six
months shall be spent as a Law Clerk
under the supervision of the Principal
District/District and Sessions Judges
or  Members  of  the  Higher  Judicial
Services, and the remaining six months
under  the  supervision  of  a  sitting
Judge of the concerned High Court; 
(v) The aforesaid one-year period of Law
Clerkship  shall  also  be  treated  as
equivalent to one year of practice at
the Bar for the purposes of satisfying
the three-year requirement;
(vi) During the period of Law Clerkship, the
Trainee Judicial Officer shall be paid
same emoluments as admissible during
their  training  in  State  Judicial
Academies, with no further allowance,
etc.; and
(vii) Upon completion of the Law Clerkship,
the sitting Judge of the High Court
under  whose  supervision  the  Trainee
Judicial  Officer  has  served  shall
submit  a  reasoned  evaluation  report
concerning  the  performance  and
suitability of the trainee. Upon such
evaluation  being  found  satisfactory,
the Trainee Judicial Officer shall be
appointed to the regular post in the
field and shall thereafter be entitled
to  the  regular  pay  scale  and  other
service benefits attached to the post. 
74. We further direct that for the period
following  the  transition  period,  i.e.,
notification/advertisement(s)  issued  on  or
after  01.04.2027,  the  following  directions
shall govern the recruitment to the post of
Civil Judge (Junior Division): 
(i) Every candidate seeking to appear for
the  examination  for  appointment  as
Civil  Judge  (Junior  Division)  shall
possess at least one year of actual
practice  from  the  source  and
certification as laid down in direction
(vii) and (viii) of the Judgment under
Review for now and thereafter as laid
down hereinafter. Such practice shall
be  subject  to  verification  through
issuance of a Certificate of Practice,
5

--- Page 55 ---
which shall not be issued unless the
candidate’s presence and participation
in  effective  judicial  proceedings,
whether along with a senior member of
the Bar with at least 10 years practice
or otherwise, have been duly recorded
in accordance with the mechanism to be
prescribed by the High Courts; 
(ii) The  application  of  a  candidate  for
recruitment  to  the  judicial  service
from the aforesaid recruitment cycle
shall  be  entertained  only  upon
submission of the requisite certificate
evidencing completion of one year of
actual practice in the District Courts;
and
(iii) Candidates selected pursuant to such
recruitment  shall,  notwithstanding
their  one  year  of  prior  practice,
undergo the same one-year period of
intensive  training  at  the  State
Judicial Academy followed by 6 months
of Law Clerkship under the Principal
District/District and Sessions Judges
or  Members  of  the  Higher  Judicial
Services  and  thereafter  another  6
months of Law Clerkship under a sitting
Judge of the concerned High Court, in
terms of directions (ii) to (vii) of
Paragraph 73.
75. For abundant caution, we reiterate that all
notification/advertisement(s)  issued  already
after the Judgment under Review was pronounced,
as well as all notification/advertisement(s) to
be issued hereafter, for recruitment to the
post of Civil Judge (Junior Division) shall
conform  to  and  will  be  governed  by  the
aforesaid directions.
76. The State Governments, in consultation with
respective jurisdictional High Courts, shall
undertake  and  officially  notify  necessary
amendments in the applicable Rules forthwith,
and in any case, within three months from the
date  of  this  Judgment.  The  interim  orders
passed  in  these  proceedings  shall  stand
vacated.
 
77. We may also observe that the directions
issued hereinabove should adequately address
the grievances expressed by the Petitioners in
6

--- Page 56 ---
Writ  Petition  (Civil)  No.  1110/2025,
particularly  with  regard  to  the  need  for
appropriate  accommodation  for  persons  with
disabilities.
78.  In  our  considered  opinion,  it  merits
clarification that the scheme formulated herein
should  not  be  made  immutable.  Judicial
recruitment is an evolving process, and the
effect  of  the  present  arrangement  can  be
properly assessed only after it has operated
for a reasonable period. A period of three
years would provide sufficient institutional
experience to evaluate whether the combination
of limited prior practice, structured training
and  supervised  clerkship  is  achieving  the
desired objective. This Court may thereafter
revisit the scheme on the basis of material
concerning  the  quality  of  recruitment,
performance of trainees, adequacy of training,
and other relevant indicators.
79.  Therefore,  scheme  contained  in  these
directions shall remain in force for a period
of five years from the date of this judgment.
80. Upon completion of the aforesaid period of
five  years,  the  working  of  this  scheme,
including  the  quality  of  recruitment,  the
nature and efficacy of training and clerkship,
the performance of officers recruited under the
scheme and such other empirical material as may
be relevant, shall be placed before this Court
for its consideration, and the scheme shall
thereafter be revisited, if necessary.”
9. In light of this modification, the Petitioner has filed
the instant Writ Petition seeking recognition of her service,
relying on the benefit extended to Law Clerks by the Judgment
dated  20.05.2025  and  on  the  general  relaxation  of  the
practice requirement to one year by the Judgment dated
21.08.2026. She concedes that a Bench Clerk is not legally
equivalent to an Advocate or a Law Clerk, but contends that
her duties over many years in the District Judiciary, which
7

--- Page 57 ---
involve assisting Presiding Judges/Judicial Officers in the
discharge and administration of judicial and legal work, have
given her substantial practical experience. She therefore
urges that the nature and substance of her duties should be
examined, and that she should not be mechanically excluded on
the basis of the nomenclature of her post. Accordingly, she
seeks an individualized determination of her eligibility to
appear in the examination for the post of Civil Judge (Junior
Division).
10. We have heard learned counsel for the Petitioner at
considerable length and carefully perused the record.
11. While we fully endorse the reasons assigned by this
Court  vide Judgment dated 21.08.2026 in  Bhumika Trust v.
Union of India and Ors. , Writ Petition (Civil) No. 1110/2025
and connected matters, we are satisfied that, at present, the
Petitioner does not meet the eligibility conditions laid down
therein for the reasons elucidated below.
12. First, the practice requirement, as modified by the
Judgment  dated  21.08.2026  to  one  year,  is  strictly  a
requirement of practice at the Bar and does not include
within it the period served as a Law Clerk in any Court.
Under the modified scheme, the three-year requirement has
been staggered into three distinct components: one year of
practice at the Bar before the examination, one year of
intensive  training  at  the  State  Judicial  Academy  upon
appointment,  and  one  year  of  structured  Law  Clerkship
thereafter. Each component is designed to impart a different
8

--- Page 58 ---
kind of experience. As explained in paragraphs 67 and 70 of
the Judgment dated 21.08.2026, practice at the Bar exposes a
candidate  to  the  adversarial  process;  judicial  training
prepares  the  candidate  for  the  fundamentally  different
responsibility of adjudication; and Law Clerkship under the
direct supervision of Judges acquaints the candidate with the
manner in which court proceedings are to be conducted. The
scheme thus ensures that every candidate acquires diversified
experience of practice, training and, thereafter, clerkship
in both the District Court and the High Court, so as to be
sufficiently equipped for the duties of a judicial officer.
13. The requirement of minimum one year of practice is,
therefore, an essential and independent component of the
scheme.  It  is  the  only  component  that  precedes  the
examination, and the only one through which a candidate
experiences the working of courts from the side of the Bar.
The treatment of other forms of experience, including Law
Clerkship, at par with practice at the Bar was accepted only
as  a  one-time  measure  for  the  transitional  period,  as
specified in paragraph 66 of the Judgment dated 21.08.2026,
and finds expression in the deemed year of practice extended
to all law graduates by paragraph 73(i). For recruitment
notified on or after 01.04.2027, paragraph 74 insists upon a
certificate evidencing one year of “actual practice in the
District Courts”, and requires selected candidates to undergo
training and Law Clerkship “notwithstanding their one year of
prior practice”.
9

--- Page 59 ---
14. The reference in paragraph 74(i) to “the source and
certification as laid down in direction (vii) and (viii) of
the Judgment dated 20.05.2025” must be understood in this
context. It adopts, for the time being, the framework within
which practice is to be undertaken, certified, and computed.
It does not carry with it the separate credit earlier given
to Law Clerkship within the three-year period. To count prior
clerkship towards the one year of practice would deprive a
candidate of the very exposure to the Bar that the scheme
deliberately secures, and would duplicate a component that
the candidate must, in any event, undergo. The mandatory
requirement of a minimum of one year of practice cannot,
therefore,  be  diluted  to  include  existing  clerkship
experience.
15. Second, and in any event, service as a Bench Clerk in
the  District  Judiciary  is  substantially  different  from
service as a Law Clerk in this Court or the High Courts. A
Law  Clerk  works  closely  with  a  Judge,  undertaking
comprehensive legal research, analysis of pending matters,
and some drafting. It is this close association with the
process of adjudication that led this Court, in the Judgment
dated 20.05.2025, to count experience as a Law Clerk towards
the period of practice. By contrast, the duties of a Bench
Clerk, though indispensable to the functioning of any court,
are more ministerial in nature and do not involve such close
contact  with  judicial  decision-making.  The  Petitioner
apprehends that she is being excluded merely on the basis of
10

--- Page 60 ---
the nomenclature of her post. We hasten to clarify that such
is not the case. The distinction lies in the nature and
substance of the duties discharged and, examined on that very
touchstone, her service cannot be equated with that of a Law
Clerk.
16. For the foregoing reasons, the Petitioner’s long service
as a Bench Clerk cannot be counted towards the requirement of
one  year  of  practice,  and  no  occasion  arises  for  an
individualized determination of her eligibility.
17. Consequently,  we  decline  to  entertain  this  Writ
Petition. The same is, accordingly, dismissed.
18. Pending applications, if any, also stand disposed of.

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. In continuation of previous orders, the High-Powered Enquiry  
Committee (HPEC) submitted its status report dated 06.10.2026. It  
is informed that the first meeting of the Committee was convened on  
01.09.2026 and thereafter a space to operate as the Secretariat has  
been identified, where necessary repair work is ongoing. Mr.  
Srinivasa Kammath R, a retired Joint Registrar (Law) from the  
National Human Rights Commission (NHRC), has been appointed as the  
Member Secretary of the Committee.
2. It is pointed out that the HPEC has vide its letter dated  
13.09.2026 made the following requests to the Ministry of Home  
Affairs (MHA): (i) Server of adequate capacity to be installed in  

--- Page 72 ---
11
the server room being made ready in the proposed office; (ii) a  
suitable portal developed through NIC to facilitate the aggrieved  
persons to upload their complaints; (iii) a new generator of  
adequate load bearing capacity installed at premises allotted; and  
(iv) minimum Secretariat staff comprising of 01 IT professional, 01  
Section Officer, 02 Assistant Section Officers, 02 Data Entry  
Operators, 02 Stenographers, 01 Hindi Translator and 03 Multi-
Tasking Staff (MTS).
3. With respect to the above urgent requirements of the HPEC, the  
learned Solicitor General of India assured that if any facility is  
not available so far, the needful shall be done without any delay. 
4. The  status  report  further  reveals  that  the  National  
Informatics Centre (NIC) has been engaged for the design and  
development of the portal and the two standard templates, one each  
for the aggrieved protesters and another for aggrieved police  
personnel, have been provided to the NIC along with relevant  
materials. The NIC has been instructed to ensure strict provisions  
for anonymity and confidentiality of complaints, evidence, and  
statements so as to protect vulnerable witnesses and keep their  
identities undisclosed.
5. We direct the NIC, through the MHA, to design and develop the  
portal according to the HPEC's requirements, ensuring all specified  
witness protection measures are integrated. The Committee informed  
the Court that the portal, fully equipped with all necessary  
features, is complete and scheduled for launch next week. Public  
notices will subsequently be published in major national newspapers  
to widely announce its launch.

--- Page 73 ---
12
6. As  highlighted  in  the  status  report,  certain  essential  
facilities  are  still  required  for  the  smooth  and  seamless  
functioning of the HPEC. The MHA is directed to take necessary  
steps to provide these facilities.
7. Dr. Menaka Guruswamy, learned Senior Counsel representing the  
HPEC, stated that she will inform the committee that first Action  
Taken Report in terms of the priorities highlighted in our order  
dated 10.09.2026 be submitted for further consideration.  
8. Counter affidavit, if any, be filed within three weeks.
9. List on 02.11.2026.
T.P.(Crl.) No. 836/2026
10. Since the matter pending before the Delhi High Court already  
stands transferred to this Court, we request the Hon'ble Chief  
Justice of the Delhi High Court to ensure the immediate transfer of  
records to this Court's registry, if already not transferred. The  
Secretary General of this Court will coordinate with the Registrar  
General of the Delhi High Court to facilitate a prompt transfer  
without delay.
(NITIN TALREJA)                            (PREETHI DILEEP KUMAR)

O R D E R
Heard Dr. R. M. Sharma and Mr. K. M. Nataraj, learned ASG,
appearing for the State of Uttar Pradesh.
2. IA No. 296269 of 2026 has been taken up. The same has been
filed by Dr. R. M. Sharma i.e., the person nominated by the Court
to assist with regard to the issue at hand relating to the
functioning of educational institutions, including Schools and
Colleges/Universities.
3. It has been brought to the notice of the Court that a report
in the Indian Express Newspaper dated 01.09.2026 discloses that a
fake  Medical  College  was  running  from  a  farmhouse,  which
purportedly represents that it is running various courses including
D.Pharma, B.Pharma, B.Sc. Nursing, DMLT, BMLT, BAMS, GNM and ANM.
The said institution, from the report and the photograph brought on

--- Page 81 ---
8
W.P. (C) No. 531/2025
record, appears to be running from an old dilapidated 2/3 rooms
structure, which is in the middle of a field, with no other
structure appearing to be around it. The said building is also not
maintained at all.
4. Based on the report, the local Police at Etah, in Mahmoodpur
Nagaria Village in the District of Etah, appears to have taken
action. The Police, on investigating the matter, have taken action
against one Satender Kumar, who is said to be running the said
institution, and also three other persons. One Bhagwan Das Singh
Baghel, an illiterate tea seller, was shown as the Chairman of the
institution, and upon being questioned, feigned ignorance about
what was happening. However, he admitted that he regularly used to
sign the College documents provided by Satender Kumar.
5. Mr. K.M. Nataraj, learned ASG, submits that the local Police
have acted promptly in the matter and have proceeded with the
investigation to a large extent, and the same shall also be taken
to its logical conclusion without any undue delay and trial shall
also be fast-tracked. He submits that the State has also taken
serious note of it and shall not tolerate any such activity, which
is totally against public interest.
6. We are also informed that besides the police probe, the Chief
Secretary of Uttar Pradesh has also ordered an independent inquiry
into the issue. We find that the same was required and that the
steps taken are in the public interest so that the entire fraud on
society is exposed, and based on the said inquiry future guidelines
can be issued by the UGC and all the regulatory bodies to prevent
such incidents.

--- Page 82 ---
9
W.P. (C) No. 531/2025
7. We appreciate the quick response and action taken by the State
of Uttar Pradesh in the matter.
8.  Learned counsel for the UGC submits that the moment a copy of
the Interlocutory Application was served on its counsel, they have
written  to  the  Additional  Chief  Secretary,  Higher  Education
Department, Government of Uttar Pradesh, to examine the said
matter. A copy of the letter has been produced before the Court and
is taken on record. A suggestion has come from Mr. K.M. Nataraj,
learned ASG, that the UGC, having come to know of such activity
happening  on  the  ground,  may  issue  an  advisory  to  all
authorities/institutions, and especially to the public at large,
not to take admission in or deal with institutions without first
verifying the authenticity of such institutions.
9. We find the suggestion to be useful and accordingly, a
direction is issued to the UGC and all the regulatory authorities
to issue such public advisory to act accordingly.
10.  On a query to Dr. R.M. Sharma with regard to the progress
made  by  the  Child  Safety  Monitoring  Committee,  as  had  been
constituted by the High Court of Delhi, where the matter was
pending at the relevant point of time and now stands transferred to
this Court, he has filed a sealed cover report. Having gone through
it, we refrain from dealing with the same or incorporating the
communication; however; we indicate that the Committee, with effect
from today Dr. R.M. Sharma is authorised to reconstitute the
Committee, and the said Committee may have four other members in
addition to the Chairman.
11. Accordingly,  the  matter  be  listed  on  the  date  earlier

--- Page 83 ---
10
W.P. (C) No. 531/2025
appointed i.e., 19.11.2026 at 2.00 p.m.
12. Ms. Archana Dave Pathak, learned ASG, who is appearing for the
Union of India, shall communicate the order to all the regulatory
bodies, in addition to the same being d one by the learned counsel
appearing for them in the present case. The Union of India is also
directed to monitor that these directions are duly complied with
the said Regulatory Authorities.
13.  The report submitted by Dr. R.M. Sharma, be resealed and kept
on record.
(SACHIN KUMAR SRIVASTAVA)                       (ANJALI PANWAR)
    COURT MASTER (SH)                         ASSISTANT REGISTRAR

O R D E R
Leave granted.
We  have  heard  the  learned  Senior  Counsel
appearing for the parties.
A notice was issued on 21.06.2018 for conducting
Limited Departmental Competitive Examination (LDCE)
for promotion to the post of Section Supervisor.
According to the notice, the Employee's Provident
Fund Organization (Section Supervisor) Recruitment
Regulations,  2017  ('2017  Regulations')  would  be
applicable.  Applications  were  preferred  by  the
petitioners,  before  the  Central  Administrative
Tribunal challenging the notice.  The applications
were allowed.  Aggrieved, the respondents filed writ
petitions challenging the order of the Tribunal.  The
writ petitions were allowed.  Aggrieved, the present
appeals have been preferred.

--- Page 106 ---
3
The short issue for consideration that actually
emanates for the first time before this Court in
pursuance  to  the  counter  affidavit  filed  by
respondent No.23 is as to whether the promotion would
be  governed  by  the  Employees  Provident  Fund
Organization (Section Supervisor) Recruitment Rules,
1992  or  the  '2017  Regulations'  or  the  '2017
Regulations' as revised and notified on 31.01.2019.
A specific stand has been taken by the official
respondent– respondent No.23 to the effect that the
latter Rules would govern, particularly, when 2017
Regulations have not been approved.
We shall quote the relevant paragraphs of the
counter affidavit filed by respondent No.23 which
states as under:
“ xxx  xxx xxx
9.The Recruitment Rules for the post of Section
Supervisor, 2017 were framed considering the
recommendations of Cadre Restructuring, 2016
wherein a separate post of Sr. SSA (Level-6)
was proposed.  Accordingly, the RRs for Section
Supervisor, 2017 (Level-7) were framed with a
provision for promotion from the feeder post of
Sr.SSA. However, the proposal for the creation
of Sr.SSA as a separate cadre was not approved
in further deliberations, and the post of SSA
(Level-4) remained the feeder post. As Sr. SSA
was not an approved cadre, the RRs of 2017 were
re-examined, amended with the approval of the
Government,  and  re-notified  on  31.01.2019,

--- Page 107 ---
4
wherein SSAs with 9 years of regular service
were made eligible to appear for the LDCE.
xxx  xxx xxx
17. In reply to Ground 5(B), the contents are
a matter of record.  It is further submitted
that the EPFO (Section Supervisor) Recruitment
Rules, 2017 were framed as per DoPT norms
considering  the  recommendations  of  Cadre
Restructuring, 2016 wherein a separate post of
Sr.SSA was proposed.  However, the proposal for
the creation of Sr.SSA was not approved.  Since
Sr.SSA  is  not  an  approved  cadre,  the
Recruitment Rules were thereafter revised and
re-notified  with  Government  approval  on
31.01.2019,  wherein  SSAs  with  9  years  of
regular service were made eligible for LDCE.
The CAT Chandigarh, while passing its order
dated 28.11.2019, did not quash the Recruitment
Rules of 2017.
xxx  xxx xxx
25. In reply to Ground 5(J), the contents are
denied.  The Section Supervisor Recruitment
Rules,  2017  were  subsequently  revised  and
superseded by the amended RRs notified in 2019.
Thus, the earlier eligibility provision under
the RRs of 2017, which formed the basis of the
challenge, no longer remains in force. The
amended 2019 RRs have not been specifically
challenged by the Petitioners;hence, the issue
relating to the earlier eligibility condition
does not survive in its original form.
xxx  xxx xxx  ”
In view of the above, we are inclined to set
aside the impugned judgment(s), and remit the matters

--- Page 108 ---
5
to the High Court for fresh consideration.
Taking note of the issues involved, we request
the  High  Court  to  expedite  the  hearing  of  the
matters, and make an endeavour to conclude it within
a period of six months from the date of receipt of a
copy of this order.
All issues are left open.
The appeals stand disposed of, accordingly.
Pending  application(s),  if  any,  shall  stand
disposed of.
…………………………………………..J.

…………………………………………..J.

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The State of Jharkhand, in purported exercise of its powers
under Section 46(2) and (3) read with Section 2 of the Police Act,
1861, has notified the Selection and Appointment of the Director
General and Inspector General of Police (Head of the Police Force)
6

--- Page 118 ---
Regulations, 2025. Under the amended Regulations, an officer has
been made eligible to be considered for appointment as Director
General of Police of the State even if the total service before
superannuation is less than six months, by giving credit to
retrospective service. 
2. Such a provision, prima facie, seems to be directly in teeth
of the law laid down by this Court in Prakash Singh. In the event
of the Rule being annulled, the consequential appointment of Smt.
Tadasha Mishra, Director General of Police, Jharkhand State, would
also be required to be set aside.
3. We,  therefore,  deem  it  appropriate  to  accord  her  an
opportunity of being heard, for which the State of Jharkhand is
directed to serve notice on her to enable her to appear and assist
this Court. Meanwhile, the State of Jharkhand shall be at liberty
to revisit the purportedly offending Rule. Alternatively, the State
shall be at liberty to file its counter-affidavit.
4. We have also requested Shri Raju Ramachandran, learned Senior
Counsel and  Amicus Curiae , to assist us on the validity of the
Rule(s) formulated by the State of Jharkhand with respect to
appointment of the Director General of Police. He is also similarly
requested to appraise other such statutes/Rules/Regulations and
consequential appointments made by other States as well. 
5. The Union Public Service Commission is also directed to assist
the learned Amicus Curiae in this regard.
6. The note submitted by the Union of India is taken on record.
7

--- Page 119 ---
7. Post the matters on 03.11.2026.
(ARJUN BISHT)                             (PREETHI DILEEP KUMAR)
ASTT. REGISTRAR-cum-PS                        DY. REGISTRAR

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL ORIGINAL JURISDICTION
TRANSFER PETITION (C) NO.3231/2025
NIHARIKA SINGH PETITIONER(S)
                              VERSUS
NIKESH KUMAR SINGH RESPONDENT(S)
O R D E R
1. This  Transfer  Petition  has  been  filed  by  the
Petitioner-Wife seeking transfer of Matrimonial Case No.
146/2025, which was filed by the Respondent–Husband under
Section 13(1)(i-a) of the Hindu Marriage Act, 1955 (‘HMA,
1955’) before the Principal Judge, Family Court, Jamui,
Bihar to the Court of Principal Judge, Family Court,
Ranchi, Jharkhand.
2. The parties got married on 21.01.2025 at Ranchi and
have no child born from the wedlock. It seems that soon
thereafter, differences arose between the parties owing
to which, FIR No. 412/2025 was registered at Police
Station  Jagarnathpur,  Ranchi  under  Sections  126(2),
115(2), 351(2), 352, 85 and 3(5) of the Bharatiya Nyaya
Sanhita, 2023 read with Sections 3 and 4 of the Dowry
Prohibition Act, 1961 against the Respondent-Husband and
his family members. Meanwhile, the Respondent–Husband
filed  the  above-stated  petition  for  dissolution  of
marriage, giving rise to the instant proceedings.
1

--- Page 134 ---
3. On 15.04.2026, when this matter was taken up for
hearing, learned counsel for the parties made a joint
request  to  refer  the  matter  to  the  Supreme  Court
Mediation  Centre  to  explore  the  possibility  of  an
amicable settlement.
4. Pursuant thereto, the parties have amicably resolved
their dispute and have filed a joint application under
Article  142  of  the  Constitution  of  India  read  with
Section  13-B  of  the  HMA,  1955.  Along  with  the
application, the parties have also placed on record the
Settlement Agreement dated 05.09.2026, which is duly
signed by the parties and their respective Advocates,
besides the Mediator.
5. As per the settlement, the Respondent-Husband has
agreed to pay a sum of Rs. 33,00,000/- (Rupees Thirty-
Three Lakhs only) to the Petitioner–Wife towards full and
final settlement of all her claims arising out of the
matrimonial relationship. In pursuance of the same, the
Respondent–Husband has handed over a demand draft bearing
number 000216 dated 29.09.2026 to the Petitioner–Wife,
who is present in person, for the stated amount.
6. The parties have further resolved that:
(i) Matrimonial Case No. 146/2025, the transfer whereof is
sought in the instant proceedings, may be disposed of
as withdrawn.
2

--- Page 135 ---
(ii) FIR  No.  412/2025,  dated  18.10.2025,  registered  at
Police  Station  Jagarnathpur,  Ranchi  under  Sections
126(2),  115(2),  351(2),  352,  85  and  3(5)  of  the
Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 read with Sections 3 and
4 of the Dowry Prohibition Act, 1961, and all the
proceedings arising therefrom, which are pending before
Judicial  Magistrate  First  Class-IX,  District  Court
Ranchi, Jharkhand, be quashed.
(iii) Complaint Case No. 10104/2026, filed by the Petitioner-
Wife under the Domestic Violence Act, 2005 which is
pending  before  Judicial  Magistrate  First  Class-IX,
District Court Ranchi, Jharkhand, be also quashed.
(iv) Quashing Petitions bearing Cr.M.P. Nos. 330/2026 &
331/2026,  filed  by  the  family  members  of  the
Respondent–Husband  seeking  quashing  of  FIR  No.
412/2025,  which  are  pending  before  High  Court  of
Jharkhand at Ranchi, be disposed of as withdrawn.
(v) Original Maintenance Case No.616/2025, filed by the
Petitioner-Wife, under Section 144 of the Bharatiya
Nagrik Suraksha Sanhita, 2023, which is pending before
Additional Principal Judge, Family Court-II, District
Court Ranchi, Jharkhand, be disposed of as withdrawn.
(vi) FIR  No.  471/2025,  dated  17.09.2025,  registered  at
Police  Station  Kadugodi,  Whitefield  Sub-Division,
Bengaluru City, filed by the Petitioner-Wife under
Section  305  of  the  Bharatiya  Nyaya  Sanhita,  2023
against an unknown person for theft of a scooter
bearing Registration No. KA01KJ9763 and other items
mentioned therein, be also quashed.
(vii) Non-Cognizable Case bearing NCR No. 338/2025 dated
3

--- Page 136 ---
13.08.2025, filed by the Respondent–Husband against the
Petitioner–Wife and her relatives at Police Station
Kadugodi, Bengaluru City, be also quashed. 
7. The parties are agreed to and it is stated before us
that the scooter and other articles have already been
returned.
8. Upon compliance with the above-stated conditions, the
parties have further agreed, as stated in the joint
application, that they seek dissolution of their marriage
by a decree of divorce by mutual consent.
9. The Petitioner–Wife, who is present in court, has
stated before us that the settlement has taken place with
her free will and consent.
10. In view of the fact that the parties have amicably
resolved  their  marital  dispute,  we  allow  the  joint
application,  i.e.  in  I.A.  No.308391/2026  with  the
following directions:
(i) Having regard to the fact that the parties are living
separately for more than a year and they have jointly
prayed for dissolution of their marriage by way of
mutual consent, we accept their prayer and direct
that the marriage between the parties shall stand
dissolved by a decree of divorce by mutual consent.
(ii) All the cases mentioned in paragraph 10(F) of the
joint application are hereby quashed/disposed of as
4

--- Page 137 ---
infructuous/withdrawn.
11. The parties shall abide by the terms and conditions
of the Settlement Agreement. All the terms and conditions
of the Settlement Agreement shall be treated as part of
this order.
12. The Transfer Petition is, accordingly, disposed of.
13. Pending applications, if any, also stand disposed of.
..........................CJI.
(SURYA KANT)
............................J.
(JOYMALYA BAGCHI)

(V. MOHANA)

    IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
 CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CIVIL APPEAL No(s).   of 2026
Arising out SLP(C) No(s). 8221/2025
KUMARI BABY                                    Appellant(s)
                            VERSUS
RAJASTHAN STATE ROAD TRANSPORT   Respondent(s)
CORPORATION (R.S.R.T.C.) & ORS.
     O R D E R
1. Leave granted.
2. Appellant has moved claim petition
seeking compensation of Rs. 23,02,000/- on
account of death of one, Om Prakash, who was a
labourer aged about 25 years as on the date of
the  occurrence  of  the  incident  i.e.  on
20.11.1997.
3. Basing on the evidence adduced by
both the parties in the course of inquiry
before the Motor Accident Claims Tribunal (for
short,  ‘the  Tribunal’),  an  award  of
Rs.5,45,400/- was passed on 11.10.2004 by the
Tribunal.

--- Page 145 ---
2
4. As against the said award, both the
parties preferred appeal before the High Court
in which, the matter was remanded back to the
Tribunal for a fresh inquiry and award.
5. After the matter was remanded back
to the Tribunal, the Tribunal passed an award
on  01.04.2017  directing  payment  of
Rs.6,11,600/- to the claimant(s). It is this
award  of  the  Tribunal  which  was  assailed
before the High Court wherein, the impugned
order has been passed reducing the amount of
compensation  from  Rs.6,11,600/-  to
Rs.3,36,112/-.
6. Having heard learned counsel for the
parties and in the facts and circumstances of
the case, we are of the considered view that
the High Court ought not to have reduced the
compensation  allowed  in  favour  of  the
claimant(s)/ appellant(s).
7. Accordingly,  the  impugned  order
passed by the High Court is set aside and the
award passed by the Tribunal in favour of the
claimant(s) is restored.

--- Page 146 ---
3
8. Accordingly, the appeal is allowed.
9. Pending  application(s),  if  any,
shall stand disposed of.
......................J.
  (PRASHANT KUMAR MISHRA)
......................J.

ITEM NO.4               COURT NO.16               SECTION II-A
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
Petition(s) for Special Leave to Appeal (Crl.)  No(s). 13985/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 26-02-2026
in IA No. 1709/2026 passed by the High Court of Jharkhand at
Ranchi]
VIKASH KUMAR @ VIKAS KUMAR                         Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
UNION OF INDIA                                     Respondent(s)
IA No. 207024/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.
 
Date : 09-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SATISH CHANDRA SHARMA
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE NONGMEIKAPAM KOTISWAR SINGH
For Petitioner(s) :Mr. Harsh Kaushik- 2302, AOR
                   
                   
For Respondent(s) :Mrs. Archana Pathak Dave, A.S.G.
    Mr. Divyam Aggarwal, Adv.
    Mr. Raghav Sharma, Adv.
    Dr. N. Visakamurthy- 3411, AOR
                   Ms. Astha Singh, Adv.
                   Mr. Kartikeya Asthana, Adv.
                   Ms. Vidushi Pandey, Adv.
                   Mr. Gaurang Bhushan, Adv.
                   Mr. Karunesh Kumar Shukla, Adv.
                   Mr. Arvind Kumar Sharma-aor, Adv.
                   
                   
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The petitioner has filed the present Special Leave
Petition, being aggrieved by the order dated 26.02.2026
1

--- Page 154 ---
passed by the High Court of Jharkhand at Ranchi in I.A. No.
1709 of 2026 in Cr. Appeal (DB) No. 981 of 2023, whereby
the  High  Court  rejected  the  petitioner's  prayer  for
suspension of sentence.
2. The prosecution case, in brief, is that the officers
of the Narcotics Control Bureau intercepted a red Eicher
Pro 1059 XP goods carrier bearing registration No. JH-05-V-
2371,  which  was  allegedly  being  escorted  by  a  Hero
motorcycle. The occupants of the goods carrier, as well as
the persons travelling on the motorcycle, were apprehended,
and 697 kg of ganja was allegedly recovered from the goods
carrier.
3. Learned counsel appearing for the petitioner, at the
outset,  submitted that the High Court had granted bail to
the co-accused persons who were travelling in the Eicher
goods carrier, from which the contraband was recovered. It
was further submitted that the petitioner was allegedly
escorting the goods carrier on a motorcycle and that the
co-accused  person  who  was  riding  pillion  with  the
petitioner had also been granted bail.
4. Learned counsel appearing for the respondent, Union
of  India,  did  not  dispute  the  aforesaid  factual
submissions.
5. Having considered the submissions advanced on behalf
2

--- Page 155 ---
of the parties, this Court is of the view that, in the
facts and circumstances of the present case, the petitioner
is entitled to the benefit of parity, particularly when the
co-accused persons from whose possession the contraband was
recovered,  as  well  as  the  co-accused  person  who  was
accompanying the petitioner on the motorcycle, have been
granted  bail.  It  is  also  relevant  to  note  that  the
respondent has not sought cancellation of the bail granted
to the aforesaid co-accused persons.
6. In view of the aforesaid facts and circumstances,
this  Court  is  of  the  considered  opinion  that  the
petitioner's prayer for suspension of sentence deserves to
be allowed. Accordingly, the prayer is allowed.
7. The  petitioner  is  accordingly  directed  to  be
released on bail during the pendency of the appeal before
the High Court, subject to such terms and conditions as may
be imposed by the Trial Court.
8. With  the  above  observations,  the  Special  Leave
Petition is disposed of.
9. Pending application(s), if any, shall stand disposed
of.
  (RAHUL KUMAR)                                    (KOMAL)
COURT MASTER (SH)                             COURT MASTER (NSH)
3

--- Page 156 ---

          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The  petitioner before  this Court  has filed  the
present petition, being aggrieved by the impugned order
dated 18.05.2026 passed by the High Court of Punjab and
Haryana at Chandigarh in CRMM No. 520 of 2026, rejecting
the prayer for anticipatory bail in connection with FIR No.
1

--- Page 157 ---
161 of 2025, registered at Police Station Sadar Kapurthala,
District  Kapurthala,  for  the  offence  punishable  under
Section 15 of the NDPS Act1. 
2. This Court, vide order dated 01.06.2026, granted
interim protection to the petitioner in the matter.
3. The allegation against the petitioner is that he is
the registered owner of the vehicle used for illegally
transporting 120 kg of poppy husk.
4. Learned counsel for the petitioner contends that the
petitioner is not named in the FIR, no recovery was
effected from his conscious possession, and he had handed
over his vehicle to his friend, who might have used the
same for transporting the contraband.
5. Learned  counsel  for  the  respondent-State  has
vehemently opposed the prayer for anticipatory bail.
6. It  is  undisputed  that  pursuant  to  the  interim
protection granted by this Court, the petitioner has joined
the investigation. 
7. This Court after hearing learned counsel for the
parties and keeping in view the totality of circumstances,
is of the opinion that the interim order dated 01.06.2026
deserves to be made absolute.
8. Accordingly, interim order dated 01.06.2026 is made
1. Narcotic Drugs and Psychotropic Substances Act, 1985
2

--- Page 158 ---
absolute subject to the condition that petitioner shall
continue to co-operate with the investigation and shall
report  as  and  when  called  upon  to  do  so  by  the
Investigating Authority.
8. The  petitioner,  if  holding  any  passport,  shall
surrender the same before the Trial Court. 
10. The Special Leave Petition is accordingly disposed
of in the above terms.
11. Pending application(s), if any, shall also stand
disposed of.

1

--- Page 172 ---
2. The petitioner is facing prosecution under Section
108 of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023 (Section 306 of
the Indian Penal Code, 1860), pursuant to the suicide of
the deceased Navneet Prabhat Ekka, who on 6 th March, 2026
was found hanging in the room of his residence.  On 7 th
March, 2026, the FIR was lodged by the mother of the
deceased against the petitioner.
3. The petitioner calls in question the correctness of
the order dated 28 th July, 2026 passed by the High Court
of Jharkhand at Ranchi in ABA No. 2921/2026.  By the said
order, the petitioner’s application for anticipatory bail
was rejected in connection with FIR No. 55 of 2026 dated
7th March,  2026  registered  at  Police  Station  Kanke,
District  Ranchi, Jharkhand  for the offence punishable
under Section 108 of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita, 2023.
4. Being aggrieved, the petitioner filed the present
petition seeking anticipatory bail.  When the matter came
up on 19th August, 2026,  while issuing notice, this Court
granted interim protection to the petitioner.
5. We have heard the learned counsel appearing for the
parties  and  perused  the  counter  affidavit  filed  by
respondent-State stating therein that the petitioner is
cooperating with the investigation.
2

--- Page 173 ---
6. Considering the overall facts and circumstances of
the case and the nature of the offence,  we confirm the
interim protection granted to the petitioner by this
Court on 19th August, 2026.  The petitioner shall continue
to cooperate with the investigation.

above terms.

1. Perused the office report dated 28th September, 2026.
2. As per the Office Report, the review petition(s) was
found defective and the reasons thereof were pointed out in
the said Report.  Notwithstanding the defects pointed out

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
MISCELLANEOUS APPLICATION NO.       OF 2026
[Diary No(s).52659/2026]
IN
CIVIL APPEAL NO.6028/2026
INCOME TAX OFFICER APPELLANT/NON-APPLICANT
                              VERSUS
PRADEEP KUMAR GARG RESPONDENT/APPLICANT
O R D E R
1. Delay condoned.
2. This application has been moved to recall the order
dated 10.04.2026 passed in Income Tax Officer v. Tej Partap
Singh, Diary No. 2196 of 2026 and connected matters, only
qua C.A. No. 6028/2026, which was disposed of  vide the
above-stated order along with hundreds of other appeals.
3. We have heard learned Senior Counsel for the parties.
4. We find that the case of the applicant-respondent is
slightly distinguishable from the batch of matters that were
originally dealt with and is required to be adjudicated by
the High Court separately, in view of the fact that the case
of the respondent–assessee did not pertain to re-assessment
under Section 147 of the Income Tax Act, 1961, and instead,
concerned a fresh assessment made under Section 143(2) of
the Income Tax Act, 1961.
5. Keeping this distinction in mind, the High Court will
decide separately whether Section 147A of the Income Tax
Act, 1961, which was inserted retrospectively through the
Finance Act, 2026, shall apply to the case of the applicant-
respondent. In this regard, it is clarified that we have not
expressed any opinion on merits. Both the parties will raise
1

--- Page 199 ---
their respective contentions before the High Court.
6. The Miscellaneous Application is, accordingly, disposed
of.

(SURYA KANT)
............................J.
(JOYMALYA BAGCHI)  
............................J.
(V. MOHANA)

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CRIMINAL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CRIMINAL APPEAL NO.  1901/2014
THE STATE OF RAJASTHAN                             Appellant(s)
                                VERSUS
MANOJ KUMAR @ MANA RAM                             Respondent(s)
O R D E R
1. The present appeal is arising out of judgment dated
23.11.2013 passed by the High Court of judicature for Rajasthan
at Jodhpur.
2. The facts of the case reveals that the respondent before
this Court was convicted vide judgment dated 29.11.2005 by the
Additional Sessions Judge, Fast Track Court No. 1, Jodhpur in
Sessions Case No. 118/2004 and sentenced for the offences
punishable under Section 302 of IPC. He was awarded life
imprisonment along with a fine of Rs. 5,000/- with a default
clause of rigorous imprisonment for three years.
3. The facts of the case is that the incident took place on
19.05.2004 at 06:15 pm.  The deceased was assaulted by the
accused person and based upon the statement of eyewitnesses,
the trial court has awarded life imprisonment, however, the
High  Court  has  held  the  respondent  guilty  for  offences
punishable under Section 304 Part 1 of the Indian Penal Code
and the sentence has been reduced from life imprisonment to 10

--- Page 208 ---
2
years along with a fine of Rs. 5,000/-.  The High Court has
noticed the discrepancies brought to its notice and the view
expressed  by  the  High  Court  is  a  plausible  view.   The
respondent has already undergone the sentence awarded by the
High Court and, therefore, at this stage, keeping in view the
judgment of the High Court, we do not find any reason to
interfere.  
5. Accordingly, the Criminal Appeal is dismissed.
6. Pending applications, if any, shall stand disposed of.
…………………………………………………………………. J.
 (SATISH CHANDRA SHARMA)
………………..…………………………………………………………. J.
 (NONGMEIKAPAM KOTISWAR SINGH)
New Delhi,
7th October, 2026

IN THE SUPREME COURT OF INDIA
CIVIL APPELLATE JURISDICTION
CIVIL APPEAL NO. 565/2016
NADIAD MUNCIPALITY                 Appellant(s)
                                VERSUS
THE STATE OF GUJARAT & ORS.                        Respondent(s)
O R D E R
1. The appellant Nadiad Municipality Corporation before this
Court is aggrieved by the order dated 17.10.2011 passed by the
High Court of Gujarat at Ahmedabad in Letters Patent Appeal
(LPA)  No.  1145/2002  by  which  the  LPA  preferred  by  the
Municipality Corporation was dismissed. The facts of the case
reveal that the respondents raised a dispute claiming permanent
status and continuity in service w.e.f. 01.01.2000 and the
Industrial Tribunal in reference I.T No. 141/1998 passed an
award on 28.08.2001, partly accepting the claim of 21 workmen
and they were directed to be made permanent w.e.f. 01.01.2000
and as well as the benefit of continuity in service was
granted.
2. It is an undisputed fact that the claim made by the
respondent Union/workmen remain unchallenged meaning thereby
the Municipality Corporation did not examine any witnesses nor
did it produce any evidence before the Tribunal and in absence
of any effective defense an award was passed on 28.08.2001.

--- Page 211 ---
2
3. The award was challenged before the High Court of Gujarat
and the learned Single Judge vide order dated 26.03.2002 passed
in Special Civil Application No. 3499/2012 has dismissed the
Writ Petition summarily against which the LPA was preferred and
the Division Bench of the High Court of Gujarat has dismissed
the LPA also.
4. It is pertinent to note that the Review Petition was also
preferred in the matter and it was also dismissed vide order
dated 04.10.2013.
5. This court does not find any reason to interfere with
finding of facts arrived at by the Industrial Tribunal vide
order dated 28.08.2001 and also does not find any reason to
interfere with the order passed by the High Court.
6. Accordingly, the Civil Appeal is dismissed.
7. The workers shall be entitled for all consequential benefits
as awarded by the Industrial Tribunal.
8. Pending applications, if any, shall stand disposed of.
…………………………………………………………………. J.
 (SATISH CHANDRA SHARMA)
………………..…………………………………………………………. J.
 (NONGMEIKAPAM KOTISWAR SINGH)
New Delhi,
7th October, 2026

ITEM NO.4               COURT NO.6               SECTION II-B
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
Petition for Special Leave to Appeal (Crl.)  No.8865/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated  07-05-2026
in CRMM No. 19994/2026 passed by the High Court of Punjab & Haryana
at Chandigarh]
TILAK RAJ                                          Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
STATE OF HARYANA & ANR.                            Respondent(s)
(IA No. 147004/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING C/C OF THE IMPUGNED
JUDGMENT & IA No. 147009/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.)
Date : 09-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM : HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE J.B. PARDIWALA
        HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE K. VINOD CHANDRAN
For Petitioner(s) : 
                   Ms. Tanya Srivastava, AOR
                   Mr. Gurfateh Singh Khosa, Adv.
                   Mr. Anmol Kheta, Adv.                   
For Respondent(s) : 
                   Mr. Vikramaditya Chouhan, A.A.G.
                   Mr. Samar Vijay Singh, AOR
                   Ms. Sabarni Som, Adv.
                   Mr. Aman Dev Sharma, Adv.
                   Mr. Manpreet Singh, Adv.
                   Dr. Mahesh Y Reddy, Adv.
                   Mr. Pardeep Kumar, Adv.
                   Mr. Vivek Kumar, Adv.                   
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. The petitioner has been denied anticipatory bail by the High Court
in connection with FIR No. 42/2026, registered at PS Bhupani, Faridabad,
for offences punishable under Sections 316(3), 318(4), and 61 of the
Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita (for short, “BNS”).
2. The complainant alleges that her ex-husband, co-accused Prabhat
Ranjan (“A1”), sought financial assistance of Rs.5 lakh to complete the
construction of a road with co-accused Pradeep Chaudhary (“A2”). As she

--- Page 253 ---
2
was not in a position to provide the money, A1 proposed that she obtain a
loan and mortgage her plot as security. On A1’s assurance that the loan
amount would be repaid with interest within 15 days, she signed what she
believed  to  be  mortgage  documents.  She  later  discovered  that  her
signatures had, in fact, been obtained on an agreement to sell. She
further alleges that she never received the sale consideration.
3. During  the  investigation,  it  was  allegedly  found  that  the
petitioner, along with co-accused A1, had fraudulently executed the
agreement to sell the plot for a sum of Rs.7 Lakhs.
4. Learned counsel for the petitioner challenged the impugned order on
various grounds, including that there was a delay of 2.5 years from the
date of the alleged transaction; that the dispute is purely civil and
contractual in nature; that the case is based entirely on documentary
evidence;  and  that,  as  all  relevant  records  are  either  with  the
investigating agency or in the public domain, custodial interrogation
would serve no investigative purpose and would be disproportionate to the
alleged offence.
5. On a prima facie consideration of the material on record, we are of
the  opinion that  the petitioner  can be  granted anticipatory  bail.
6. Accordingly,  we  direct  that,  in  the  event  of  his  arrest  in
connection with the offences referred to above, he shall be released on
bail, subject to such terms and conditions as the Investigating Officer
(for short, “I.O.”) may deem fit to impose and subject further to his
continued cooperation in the investigation.
7. Upon being released on bail by the I.O., the petitioner shall,
pursuant to any direction issued by the I.O. or summons issued by the
Judicial Magistrate First Class, Faridabad (the jurisdictional court),
appear before the jurisdictional court, furnish bail bonds, and ensure
due appearance during the trial.

The limited grievance of the learned counsel
appearing for the petitioners, is that despite the
writ petition being WP No.7879/2026, filed in the
month of June, 2026, the petitioners are awaiting an
urgent interim order. However, the same has not been
taken up for hearing.

--- Page 255 ---
2
The  learned  counsel  appearing  for  the
petitioners  further  submits  that  there  is  no
objection in the matter, and the adjournment orders
have been passed along with other matters, which are
totally different.
The Writ Petition is on the stage of admission,
and is yet to be taken up for hearing for the first
time.
Taking  into  consideration  the  above  facts,
particularly, that the petitioners are seeking an
urgent relief on the premise that they might face
eviction, we request the High Court to take up WP
No.7879/2026 for admission within a period of one
week from the date of receipt of a copy of this
order.
Accordingly, the Special Leave Petition stands
disposed of in aforesaid terms.
Pending  application(s),  if  any,  shall  stand
disposed of.

1

--- Page 279 ---

should be permitted to raise the plea of maintainability of

--- Page 341 ---

petition on its own merits. It is further clarified that we
have not expressed any opinion on the submissions of Mr.
Dama Seshadri Naidu, learned senior counsel.

For Respondent(s) : 
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Delay condoned.
2. The petitioners are serving and former members of Permanent
Lok Adalat constituted across various States. They have been/were
appointed under Section 22B(2)(b) of the Legal Services Authorities
Act, 1987. Their grievances pertain to the lack of structured and
dignified pay scale under the Permanent Lok Adalat (Other Terms and
Conditions of Appointment of Chairman and Other Persons) Rules,
2003, as amended from time to time.
3.  Having heard learned Senior Counsel for the petitioners, we
1

--- Page 397 ---
deem it appropriate to treat this writ petition as a common
representation on behalf of the petitioners and other members of
the Permanent Lok Adalats.
4. The Registry is directed to forward a copy of the writ
petition  to  the  Member  Secretary,  National  Legal  Services
Authority, who in turn, is directed to place the same before the
Central Council of NALSA, after obtaining appropriate orders from
the Executive Chairman of NALSA, for consideration of the disparity
issue raised by the petitioners. It will be appreciated if the
NALSA takes an appropriate decision in the matter at the earliest
and preferably within four months.
5. The Writ Petition is, accordingly, disposed of.
6. Pending applications, if any, also stand disposed of.

RAM NARAYAN VISHNOI                                Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
DEVENDRA KUMAR (DECEASED)& ANR.                    Respondent(s)
O R D E R
1. Application seeking oral hearing of the Review Petition is
rejected.
2. This review petition has been filed by the petitioner seeking
review of the Order dated 17th August, 2026 passed in the captioned
Special Leave Petition whereby the said petition was dismissed.
3. We have carefully gone through the said Order and the record.
In our opinion, no case for review is made out. Consequently, the

ITEM NO.8               COURT NO.1               SECTION XV
               S U P R E M E  C O U R T  O F  I N D I A
                       RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS
SPECIAL LEAVE PETITION (CIVIL)……………….. Diary No(s).51815/2026
[Arising out of impugned final judgment and order dated 24-04-2026
in DBCWP No.8319/2026 passed by the High Court of Judicature for
Rajasthan at Jodhpur]
AMRA RAM BHATI & ORS.                              Petitioner(s)
                                VERSUS
THE STATE OF RAJASTHAN & ORS.                      Respondent(s)
IA  No.  271364/2026  -  CONDONATION  OF  DELAY  IN  FILING,  IA
No.271370/2026 - EXEMPTION FROM FILING O.T.
 
Date : 09-10-2026 This matter was called on for hearing today.
CORAM :  HON'BLE THE CHIEF JUSTICE
         HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE JOYMALYA BAGCHI
         HON'BLE MRS. JUSTICE V. MOHANA
For Petitioner(s) : Mr. Sunny Choudhary- 2071, AOR
 Mr. Karan Bishnoi, Adv.
                   
For Respondent(s) : 
          UPON hearing the counsel the Court made the following
                             O R D E R
1. Delay condoned.
2. Learned counsel for the petitioners, at the outset, fairly
states that the Elections have meanwhile been held. He further
submits, and rightly so, that liberty may be granted to the
petitioners to submit objections, which may be considered by the
competent Authority in accordance with law. Ordered accordingly.
3. The Special Leave Petition is, accordingly, disposed of with
